{"type":"tool.execution_complete","data":{"toolCallId":"call_MHxBalJJSHd3NHVLREM3VGtINUM","model":"gemini-3.5-flash","interactionId":"b9af5bc3-4e64-44cf-b9a1-1eb88955489c","turnId":"8","success":true,"result":{"content":"{\"type\":\"output_text\",\"text\":{\"value\":\"Migrating from React Hook Form (RHF) v6 to v7 involves several breaking changes, **especially around the `register` function and error handling**. Here’s a guide focusing on **register** and **errors**:\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 1. `register` API Change\\n\\n### **v6:**\\nYou attached validation right in the register function:\\n\\n```js\\n<input\\n  name=\\\"email\\\"\\n  ref={register({ required: 'Email is required', pattern: /.+@.+\\\\..+/ })}\\n/>\\n```\\n\\n### **v7:**\\n- `register` is now a function returning props.\\n- Use `...register(\\\"email\\\", { required: \\\"Email is required\\\", pattern: /.+@.+\\\\..+/ })`\\n- The `ref` prop is no longer passed to the input.\\n\\n**Example:**\\n\\n```js\\n<input\\n  {...register(\\\"email\\\", { \\n    required: \\\"Email is required\\\", \\n    pattern: { value: /.+@.+\\\\..+/, message: \\\"Invalid email\\\" } \\n  })}\\n/>\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 2. Error Structure Changes\\n\\n- **v6:** Errors were accessed as `errors.inputName.type` and `errors.inputName.message` (sometimes `errors.inputName.ref`, etc).\\n- **v7:** Error object is *flattened*. Always access error by `errors.inputName?.message`.\\n\\n**Example:**\\n\\n```js\\n// In both v6 and v7\\n{errors.email && <span>{errors.email.message}</span>}\\n```\\nBut in v7, `errors.email` will now always be an object containing at least a `.type` and potentially a `.message`.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 3. Example Conversion\\n\\n**v6:**\\n\\n```js\\n<input \\n  name=\\\"username\\\"\\n  ref={register({ required: \\\"Username is required\\\" })}\\n/>\\n{errors.username && <span>{errors.username.message}</span>}\\n```\\n\\n**v7:**\\n\\n```js\\n<input \\n  {...register(\\\"username\\\", { required: \\\"Username is required\\\" })}\\n/>\\n{errors.username && <span>{errors.username.message}</span>}\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 4. Summary of Main Changes\\n\\n- `register` is no longer used as a ref; you spread the returned object (`{...register(\\\"name\\\")}`).\\n- Error objects are accessed the same, but the internal structure is simplified. It returns several values and objects to help handle form state, validation, and interaction.\\n\\nHere’s what you need to know about the **return values relevant to errors and form state** in v7:\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 1. `errors`\\n\\n- Located in: `formState.errors`\\n- **Type:** `{ [K in FieldName]?: FieldError }`\\n- **Purpose:** Contains validation errors for each input in your form.  \\n- **Example:**\\n  ```js\\n  const { formState: { errors } } = useForm();\\n  {/* Showing an error message for a field */}\\n  {errors.email && <span>{errors.email.message}</span>}\\n  ```\\n- **Note:** In v7, errors are no longer returned directly from `useForm()` but via `formState.errors`!\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 2. `formState`\\n\\n- **Type:** object\\n- **Purpose:** Contains several properties that reflect the current state of the form:  \\n    - `isDirty`, `isValid`, `isSubmitted`, `touchedFields`, `dirtyFields`, `errors` (as above), etc.\\n- **Usage:**\\n  ```js\\n  const { formState } = useForm();\\n  console.log(formState.isSubmitting);\\n  ```\\n- **Accessing Errors:**\\n  ```js\\n  const { formState: { errors } } = useForm();\\n  ```\\n  Or as a one-liner:\\n  ```js\\n  const { formState } = useForm();\\n  formState.errors\\n  ```\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 3. **Full Type Example**\\n\\n```js\\nconst { \\n  register,\\n  handleSubmit,\\n  formState: { errors, isDirty, touchedFields, isValid }\\n} = useForm();\\n```\\n- `register`: function to link form fields to RHF\\n- `handleSubmit`: function to handle form submission\\n- `formState`: an object that contains `errors` and other status/flags\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 4. **Minimal Usage Example**\\n\\n```jsx\\nimport { useForm } from 'react-hook-form';\\n\\nfunction MyForm() {\\n  const { \\n    register, \\n    handleSubmit, \\n    formState: { errors, isDirty }\\n  } = useForm();\\n\\n  return (\\n    <form onSubmit={handleSubmit(data => console.log(data))}>\\n      <input {...register(\\\"email\\\", { required: \\\"Email is required\\\" })} />\\n      {errors.email && <p>{errors.email.message}</p>}\\n      <button type=\\\"submit\\\" disabled={!isDirty}>Submit</button>\\n    </form>\\n  );\\n}\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n### **Summary Table**\\n\\n| Property                | Where to access (v7)          | Description                   |\\n|-------------------------|-------------------------------|-------------------------------|\\n| `errors`                | `formState.errors`            | Validation errors per field   |\\n| `formState`             | Return of `useForm()`         | State about the form & errors |\\n\\n---\\n\\n> **Docs:**  \\n> - register a field manually by calling the `register` method with a field name **inside a `useEffect`**, and optionally assign a value using `setValue`.\\n\\n```jsx\\nimport React, { useEffect } from {\\n  register('customField');\\n}, [register]);\\n\\n<CustomInput onChange={value => setValue('customField', value)} />\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 3. **Points to Remember**\\n- **Manual registration** Here are straightforward methods and best practices:\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 1. **Manual Registration via `useEffect`**\\n\\nFor fields **not directly compatible** with React Hook Form (like some custom UI widgets), manually registering and updating their value is common.\\n\\n```jsx\\nimport { useEffect } from 'react';\\nimport { useForm } from 'react-hook-form';\\n\\nfunction CustomInput() {\\n  const { register, setValue, unregister } = useForm();\\n\\n  useEffect(() => {\\n    // Register the field on mount\\n    register('customField', { required: true });\\n\\n    // Optionally unregister on unmount\\n    return () => unregister('customField');\\n  }, [register, unregister]);\\n\\n  const handleChange = (e) => {\\n    setValue('customField', e.target.value, { shouldValidate: true });\\n  };\\n\\n  return (\\n    <input onChange={handleChange} />\\n  );\\n}\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 2. **Render Props: Passing Register Directly to a Custom Component**\\n\\nIf your custom component accepts a `ref` and other props, pass `register()` to it (example: `register('myField')`). If not, use the `Controller` component.\\n\\n```jsx\\n// Pass register to your custom input if it forwards ref, e.g.\\n<input {...register('customField')} />\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 3. **Using `Controller` for More Control**\\n\\nFor truly custom inputs, the `Controller` component is more flexible:\\n\\n```jsx\\nimport { useForm, Controller } from 'react-hook-form';\\n\\nfunction MyComponent() {\\n  const { control } = useForm();\\n\\n  return (\\n    <Controller\\n      name=\\\"customField\\\"\\n      control={control}\\n      defaultValue=\\\"\\\"\\n      render={({ field }) => (\\n        <CustomInput {...field} />\\n      )}\\n    />\\n  );\\n}\\n```\\n- Here, `field` includes `value`, `onChange`, and `ref`, matching what React Hook Form expects.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### **Summary Table**\\n\\n| Scenario                                    | Best approach                   |\\n|----------------------------------------------|---------------------------------|\\n| Standard HTML input                         | register directly in JSX        |\\n| Custom component with ref support           | pass {...register('name')}      |\\n| Custom (no ref, complex event handling)     | use Controller                  |\\n| Fully manual field                          | use useEffect + register/unregister & setValue |\\n\\n---\\n\\n**Key points:**\\n- Call `register` **once** in render or via `useEffect`.\\n- Update the value with `setValue`.\\n- **Unregister** in `useEffect` cleanup (optional, but prevents memory leaks for dynamic fields).\\n\\n**Docs:**  \\n- Import and Setup\\n\\n```jsx\\nimport { useForm } from \\\"react-hook-form\\\";\\nimport { useEffect } from \\\"react\\\";\\n```\\n\\n### 2. Register Manually in useEffect\\n\\n```jsx\\nconst { register, setValue, unregister, handleSubmit } = useForm();\\n\\nuseEffect(() => {\\n  // Register the field\\n  register(\\\"fieldName\\\");\\n\\n  // Optionally, set the default value\\n  setValue(\\\"fieldName\\\", \\\"default value\\\");\\n\\n  // Optional: clean up/unregister when component unmounts\\n  return () => {\\n    unregister(\\\"fieldName\\\");\\n  };\\n}, [register, setValue, unregister]);\\n```\\n\\n**Key Points:**\\n- Calling `register(\\\"fieldName\\\")` inside `useEffect` will register the field.\\n- If you unregister on cleanup, the field will be deregistered once the component unmounts.\\n- If using React Hook Form v7 or later, `register` **no longer returns a ref**, so you can just call it as a function.\\n- Pass the dependencies (`register`, `setValue`, etc.) to the dependency array.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### Example: Full Usage\\n\\n```jsx\\nfunction Example() {\\n  const { register, setValue, unregister, handleSubmit } = useForm();\\n\\n  useEffect(() => {\\n    register(\\\"customField\\\");\\n    setValue(\\\"customField\\\", \\\"hello\\\");\\n    return () => unregister(\\\"customField\\\");\\n  }, [register, setValue, unregister]);\\n\\n  const onSubmit = data => {\\n    console.log(data);\\n  };\\n\\n  return <form onSubmit={handleSubmit(onSubmit)}>\\n    {/* There is no <input> for 'customField', but it's in the form data */}\\n    <button type=\\\"submit\\\">Submit</button>\\n  </form>;\\n}\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n### Docs\\n- [register - Import and Setup\\n\\n```jsx\\nimport { useForm } from \\\"react-hook-form\\\";\\nimport { useEffect } from \\\"react\\\";\\n```\\n\\n### 2. Register Manually in useEffect\\n\\n```jsx\\nconst { register, setValue, unregister, handleSubmit } = useForm();\\n\\nuseEffect(() => {\\n  // Register the field\\n  register(\\\"fieldName\\\");\\n\\n  // Optionally, set the default value\\n  setValue(\\\"fieldName\\\", \\\"default value\\\");\\n\\n  // Optional: clean up/unregister when component unmounts\\n  return () => {\\n    unregister(\\\"fieldName\\\");\\n  };\\n}, [register, setValue, unregister]);\\n```\\n\\n**Key Points:**\\n- Calling `register(\\\"fieldName\\\")` inside `useEffect` will register the field.\\n- If you unregister on cleanup, the field will be deregistered once the component unmounts.\\n- If using React Hook Form v7 or later, `register` **no longer returns a ref**, so you can just call it as a function.\\n- Pass the dependencies (`register`, `setValue`, etc.) to the dependency array.\\n\\n---\\n\\n### Example: Full Usage\\n\\n```jsx\\nfunction Example() {\\n  const { register, setValue, unregister, handleSubmit } = useForm();\\n\\n  useEffect(() => {\\n    register(\\\"customField\\\");\\n    setValue(\\\"customField\\\", \\\"hello\\\");\\n    return () => unregister(\\\"customField\\\");\\n  }, [register, setValue, unregister]);\\n\\n  const onSubmit = data => {\\n    console.log(data);\\n  };\\n\\n  return <form onSubmit={handleSubmit(onSubmit)}>\\n    {/* There is no <input> for 'customField', but it's in the form data */}\\n    <button type=\\\"submit\\\">Submit</button>\\n  </form>;\\n}\\n```\\n\\n---\\n\\n### Docs\\n- [register - React Hook Form v7 Docs](https://react-hook-form.com/docs/v7/registration/)\\n- [FAQs: How to register fields manually?](https://react-hook-form.com/faqs/#HowcanIregisterinputsmanually)\\n\\nLet me know if you need an example with controlled inputs or more complex logic!\",\"annotations\":null},\"bing_searches\":null,\"annotations\":null}","contents":[{"type":"text","text":"{\n  \"type\": \"output_text\",\n  \"text\": {\n    \"value\": \"When using **React Hook Form v7**, the `register` function is used to register an input or field into React Hook Form.
